2026-08-11 - Added verified exposure-window tracking to the GMGN browser-capture extension and its ingestion path, to close the "no continuous coverage evidence" gap flagged in the prior integrity-vs-contract review. Files changed: extension/background.js (tracks capture-session start/stop as coverageWindows in chrome.storage.local, sends a 1-minute heartbeat alarm while active, and on service-worker/browser wake splits any window whose heartbeat gap exceeds 3x the interval with closedReason='heartbeat-gap-detected-on-wake' instead of silently spanning the gap), extension/manifest.json (added "alarms" permission, bumped to 0.2.0), extension/popup.js/popup.html (show coverage window count, updated description). Files changed (app): src/db/schema.ts (migration 7: gmgn_browser_coverage_windows table), src/gmgn/browserImport.ts (parses optional coverageWindows from the export, stores one row per window, returns coverageWindowsImported; malformed window entries are dropped, not fatal), src/db/integrity.ts (new browserCoverage section: totalWindows/openWindows/gapClosedWindows/totalCoveredSeconds/windows, exposed via existing GET /api/integrity), ui/main.tsx (browser-import panel now shows coverage window count). Files added: none (extended existing files). Tests changed: tests/gmgn-browser-import.test.ts (+3 tests: window storage, duplicate-file reuse, malformed-window tolerance), tests/integrity.test.ts (+1 test for covered-duration math and gap-vs-clean-close distinction, updated 2 existing assertions for the new field), tests/schema.test.ts (updated expected table list). Decision: kept the export format backward-compatible (formatVersion still 1, coverageWindows optional) so already-captured exports still import; chose heartbeat+alarm over "trust the stop event" because MV3 service workers and Chrome itself can die without ever sending SET_ACTIVE(false). Agent: Claude Sonnet 5. Test result: npx tsc --noEmit clean (both tsconfig.json and tsconfig.ui.json), npm run build clean (server + vite), npm test 47/47 passing, verified live against the running dev server (GET /api/integrity now returns browserCoverage, currently all-zero since the one existing browser-import batch predates this feature). Errors/unresolved: the real Chrome extension has still not been loaded/tested by the user in actual Chrome (syntax-checked only via node --check); the existing single browser-import batch has no coverage windows recorded (expected, predates the feature) so verified-exposure evidence starts at zero until a new capture+export+import cycle is run. Next step: user should reload the unpacked extension in Chrome, run a capture session, export, and import it so real coverage windows start accumulating toward the v2 contract's approval gate.
2026-08-11 - Built targeted Dune enrichment (reversed workflow): export GMGN-observed token addresses first, look them up in Dune, then import just those results as a separate provenance-tagged batch, instead of relying on the broad historical cohort export. Triggered by confirming live that the Dune cohort (first-trade window 2026-07-01 to 2026-07-07) and GMGN's capture window (2026-08-10 to 2026-08-11) don't overlap at all, which is why only 1 of 370 signals matched the cohort — not a bug, a real ~5-week gap between the two datasets' time windows. Files added: src/gmgn/tokenAddresses.ts (listGmgnTokenAddresses: unique GMGN-observed addresses split into matched-to-cohort vs unmatched, since only the unmatched ones are worth a fresh Dune lookup), tests/gmgn-token-addresses.test.ts. Files changed: src/dune/importer.ts (importDuneContent/importDuneFile take an optional tokenSource param, defaulting to 'dune', so enrichment rows can be tagged 'dune-targeted-enrichment' while reusing all existing parsing/audit/ON-CONFLICT-DO-NOTHING logic unmodified), src/scripts/server.ts (new GET /api/gmgn/token-addresses, new POST /api/import-dune-enrichment archiving into .data/archive/dune-enrichment separately from the cohort archive), src/db/integrity.ts (new tokensBySource breakdown so cohort vs enrichment provenance stays visible in the integrity report), ui/main.tsx (new "TARGETED ENRICHMENT" panel in the Imports tab: export-addresses button that downloads a .txt, plus an upload dropzone for the Dune lookup result). Tests changed: tests/dune-importer.test.ts (+1 test proving enrichment never overwrites an existing cohort row and is tagged with its own source), tests/integrity.test.ts (+1 test for tokensBySource). Decision: reused the existing tokens table and Dune importer entirely rather than building a parallel enrichment schema, since ON CONFLICT(token_address) DO NOTHING already gives the required "never overwrite the original cohort" guarantee for free; provenance is distinguished by the existing tokens.source column plus an archive subfolder, not a new table. Agent: Claude Sonnet 5. Test result: npx tsc --noEmit clean (both tsconfig.json and tsconfig.ui.json), npm run build clean, npm test 51/51 passing, verified live end-to-end in a fresh browser tab against the real running server: GET /api/gmgn/token-addresses correctly returned 197 unmatched addresses (of 198 total unique GMGN addresses, 1 already in cohort), clicking "Export GMGN token addresses" in the UI fired the real request (200 OK) and updated the status message correctly with no console errors. Errors/unresolved: the reverse import side (POST /api/import-dune-enrichment) was verified by unit test and type-check but not yet exercised with a real Dune lookup result file end-to-end in the browser, since the user hasn't run a real targeted Dune query yet. Next step: user runs the exported address list through Dune, uploads the result via the new "Choose a Dune lookup result" control, and I (or they) can then check /api/integrity's tokensBySource and dataQuality.coveragePercent to see how much the match rate improves.
2026-08-11 - Added a copyable Dune SQL query generator to the TARGETED ENRICHMENT panel, so the user doesn't have to hand-write a query around the exported GMGN address list. Files changed: ui/main.tsx (buildDuneEnrichmentQuery: pure client-side template that embeds the fetched GMGN-observed addresses into a `with target_tokens (values ...)` CTE joined against dex_solana.trades, deliberately outputting columns named token_address/symbol/first_trade_time/first_dex/first_tx so a CSV/JSON export of the query result uploads back through the existing importer's aliases with zero extra mapping; new "Generate Dune query" button reuses GET /api/gmgn/token-addresses, renders the query in a readonly textarea, and a "Copy query" button uses navigator.clipboard with a manual-select fallback message if clipboard access fails), ui/styles.css (.dune-query-block textarea/button sizing). No backend or schema changes — this is presentation-only, built entirely from data the existing endpoint already returns. Decision: picked dex_solana.trades as the template table since it's a common real Dune Solana DEX table and the shape matches the cohort's original columns, but called out in an inline SQL comment that the user should adjust table/column names to match their own Dune workspace since I can't run or verify against Dune myself. Agent: Claude Sonnet 5. Test result: npx tsc -p tsconfig.ui.json --noEmit clean, npm run build clean, npm test 51/51 passing (no test changes needed, this is UI-only). Verified live end-to-end in a fresh browser tab against the real running server: clicking "Generate Dune query" fetched the real 197 addresses (200 OK) and rendered an 11,673-character query in the textarea; clicking "Copy query" successfully wrote to the clipboard and updated the status message; only console error present was an unrelated favicon 404. Errors/unresolved: the generated SQL itself has not been run against real Dune (no Dune access from here) — table/column names are a best-effort template, not a verified-working query; user should confirm/adjust against their own Dune workspace before relying on it. Next step: user runs the generated query in Dune, exports the result, and uploads it via "Choose a Dune lookup result" to complete the enrichment loop.

Finding: each completed run is retained; run 1 and run 3 were byte-value consistent, run 2 differed in 12 checkpoint prices, run 5 differed in 91 of 125 rows after later Dune data changes, and current-query runs 6 and 7 differed in 18 of 75 rows despite the same matched trade timestamps. Run 4 remained submitted without a result. Decision: the screenshots contained a separate React table-cell placement bug (now fixed), but the raw Dune provider data is also not immutable between executions and must be treated as versioned observations. Test result: read-only audit completed; no data modified. Errors/unresolved: no automatic consistency report UI yet. Next step: use archive IDs, SQL text, execution IDs, and SHA-256 values when comparing future runs.
2026-08-11 - Added concrete evidence for the Dune consistency finding. Read-only audit confirms runs 6 and 7 have identical signal_ids, identical query SQL SHA-256 `7a8c68edd3d0484293fda29ab45892a8aeb0a59bffe616c02f9c0899607e5065`, and different raw prices: signal 2701 +1h changed from 0.00001352378998014196 to 0.000013592207250417279 while matched_trade_at stayed 2026-08-11 05:59:33 UTC; signal 2702 signal price changed from 0.00002668681479593638 to 0.000026183329923903627 while matched_trade_at stayed 2026-08-11 05:19:16 UTC. Archive hashes are stored in dune_outcome_runs and the corresponding JSON archives. Decision: the claim is supported by persisted raw evidence, not inferred from the UI. Test result: read-only audit only; no data modified. Next step: expose a consistency comparison view if repeated-run auditing should be available in the UI.

2026-08-11 - Re-examined the run 6 vs run 7 Dune inconsistency (prior entries 42-43) and found the likely root cause is our own SQL, not Dune data mutating. Files inspected: src/dune/outcomes.ts (sqlFor, the ranked CTE), .data/archive/dune-outcomes/dune-outcome-6-7a6b46db43204a64.json, .data/archive/dune-outcomes/dune-outcome-7-9379dcaf945bda88.json (read-only, parsed with a one-off node -e snippet, not saved). Finding: the ranked CTE selects the matched trade with `row_number() OVER (PARTITION BY c.signal_id, c.checkpoint ORDER BY t.block_time DESC)` and no secondary tiebreaker, even though normalized_trades already carries tx_id. Solana block_time is second-granularity and an active token can have multiple trades (different pools/prices) in the same second, so ties are expected; Trino/Presto does not guarantee stable tie order across repeated executions of identical SQL text. Confirmed against the archives that for both mismatched examples (signal 2701/+1h, signal 2702/signal), matched_trade_at was byte-identical between run 6 and run 7 down to the millisecond while price_usd differed - exactly the signature of a tie-break flip (two different trades landing on the same matched second), not evidence that a stored trade value was revised. Decision made and reason: fixed sqlFor to add `t.tx_id DESC` as a secondary ORDER BY key and to carry matched_tx_id through to the final SELECT and into DuneOutcome's result shape (rowsToOutcomes), both to make the pick deterministic going forward and so a future repeat run can prove which case it is: same tx_id + different price would mean Dune itself revised a value; different tx_id would confirm it was always a tie. This entry supersedes entries 42/43's "Dune data is not immutable" interpretation with the tx_id evidence, per the append-only rule (those entries are left unmodified). Agent name and model: Claude Sonnet 5. Test result: npx tsc -b --noEmit clean (exit 0) after the change; no runtime re-execution against Dune was performed (no network call made). Errors or unresolved items: the fix is not yet exercised against a live Dune re-run of the same 25 signals, so the tx_id-based proof (same vs. different tx_id across a repeat run) is still pending; ui/main.tsx does not yet display matched_tx_id anywhere. Next step: run a fresh Dune batch twice in a row for the same signal set and confirm prices now match; if they still differ, capture the two matched_tx_id values to determine whether Dune revised the same trade or the tie-break assumption needs revisiting.
2026-08-11 - Reviewed the tx_id tie-break fix and its progress entry. Finding: adding `t.tx_id DESC` is directionally correct and the raw result now carries `matched_tx_id`, but tx_id alone is not guaranteed unique when one Solana transaction contains multiple DEX trade segments. No test currently asserts deterministic ordering or compares matched_tx_id across repeated runs, and the UI does not display the matched transaction ID. Decision: mark the fix as partially complete pending a live repeat and, ideally, adding instruction-index/pool tie-break fields. Test result: npm test 54/54 passing; no live Dune request made. Errors/unresolved: same-tx multi-segment ties remain possible. Next step: extend normalized_trades with outer_instruction_index and inner_instruction_index, order by them after tx_id, and add a repeat-consistency test/diagnostic.

2026-08-11 - Implemented the reviewer's requested instruction-index tiebreakers and validated determinism with a real live repeat run. Files changed: src/dune/outcomes.ts (normalized_trades now also selects outer_instruction_index and inner_instruction_index from dex_solana.trades in both UNION ALL branches; ranked's ORDER BY extended to `t.block_time DESC, t.tx_id DESC, t.outer_instruction_index DESC, t.inner_instruction_index DESC`; both fields carried through the final SELECT as matched_outer_instruction_index/matched_inner_instruction_index and into DuneOutcome's result shape as matchedOuterInstructionIndex/matchedInnerInstructionIndex via rowsToOutcomes). Decision made and reason: tx_id alone is not guaranteed unique per row because one Solana transaction can contain multiple routed/multi-hop DEX legs; outer/inner instruction index together identify the specific instruction within the transaction that produced a given trade row, giving a fully deterministic sort key. Validation: wrote and ran a throwaway script (adhoc_verify_determinism.ts, deleted after use, not committed) that called measureDuneOutcomes twice in direct succession against the live Dune API for the exact same 25 signal IDs used in runs 6/7/8 ([2701,2702,2703,2704,3001-3020,3024]), then diffed every (signal_id, checkpoint) pair across both runs on price_usd, matched_tx_id, matched_outer_instruction_index, and matched_inner_instruction_index. Result: both runs (persisted as dune_outcome_runs id 9 and 10, archived at .data/archive/dune-outcomes/dune-outcome-9-*.json and dune-outcome-10-*.json) returned all 75 checkpoints (25 signals x 3 checkpoints) byte-identical on every field - 0 mismatches. This directly satisfies the reviewer's approval condition ("running the same 25-signal query twice to confirm identical prices and matched trade IDs"). Agent name and model: Claude Sonnet 5. Test result: npx tsc -b --noEmit clean (exit 0); npm test 54/54 passing; live two-run determinism check above (0/75 mismatches). Errors or unresolved items: ui/main.tsx still does not display matched_tx_id or the instruction-index fields anywhere (deferred - not required for the reviewer's stated approval bar, which was determinism + a passing repeat run, not UI surfacing); no permanent automated test asserts repeat-run determinism, only the one-off manual script that was deleted after use. Next step: if ongoing regression protection is wanted, add a committed test/diagnostic that re-runs a fixed signal set (or a recorded fixture) and asserts stable ordering, since the current proof is a manual run rather than a permanent CI check.

2026-08-11 - Connected the live Patterns report to signal measurement so it updates automatically instead of only on page load/manual Refresh. User asked "shouldn't it be connected to measure all signals and get updated when the new signals are there" after I explained the report previously only refetched on mount or a manual Refresh click. Files changed: ui/main.tsx (new `refreshPatternReport` helper; `measureSelectedOutcome` calls it after a successful measurement; `measureAllOutcomes` calls it after every batch in its loop — not just at the end — so the Patterns table updates progressively as a long multi-batch run proceeds, plus once more in the catch path so a failed run still reflects whatever batches completed before it errored). Decision made and reason: the report computation itself was already cheap and local (no new Dune query, just reading already-stored data), so refetching it after every batch adds negligible cost while giving real-time feedback during a run that can take minutes; saved snapshots are deliberately left untouched by this change since they exist specifically to stay frozen. Errors or unresolved items: none. Next step: none planned.
2026-08-11 - Fixed a methodology gap found while analyzing "12 · Smart-money buy" for the user: the pattern report could not tell "price didn't move" from "no new trade happened, so the last known price was reused." Investigation (read-only, via throwaway node scripts against the live database, not committed) found 38% of type 7's and 50% of type 18's "with data" comparisons were actually the exact same matched_trade_at reused for both the signal and the horizon checkpoint - a stale no-op, not a real price observation - and that type 12's eye-catching +111% average return was driven entirely by one outlier trade (+8958% in 1h on a single pump.fun token), flipping to -41% average if just the top 3 winners are excluded; its honest median was already -43.9%, one of the worst of any type. Files changed: src/dune/outcomes.ts (DuneOutcome's checkpoint result now carries `matchedTradeAt`, previously computed by the SQL but silently dropped in `rowsToOutcomes`), src/db/patterns.ts (rewrote the comparison logic: each checkpoint pair now classifies as missing/stale/fresh via `classifyComparison`; up%/avg/median are computed only over "fresh" genuine-new-trade comparisons; added `nStale`/`nFresh` fields and a `staleNote` explaining this; groups are now ranked by median instead of average, since a single outlier can swing an average far more than a median), tests/patterns.test.ts (added a test proving a same-matched-trade checkpoint is classified stale and excluded, and a test proving an outlier-driven group is NOT ranked first despite having the higher average - the steady, better-median group must rank first), ui/main.tsx (SignalPatternGroup/Report types updated; Patterns table gained "stale"/"fresh" columns and renders `staleNote` plus a one-line explanation that ranking is by median not average). Decision made and reason: exclude stale comparisons entirely from the statistics rather than counting them as 0% change, since they carry no information about what actually happened to the price; rank by median because this session directly demonstrated average is trivially gamed by one outlier while median reflects the typical outcome. Agent name and model: Claude Sonnet 5. Test result: `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx tsc -p tsconfig.ui.json --noEmit` both clean; `npm run build` clean; `npm test` 59/59 passing (57 prior + 2 new). Verified live against the real running dev server and real database (670 signals, 42 archived Dune runs): type 7 "Price ATH" now correctly ranks first (fresh=18, up%=72.2%, median=+5.2%) after excluding its 11 stale entries; type 12 "Smart-money buy" now shows nStale=13, nFresh=49, and its already-bad median got worse (-65.4%) once the diluting stale "flat" entries were removed, while its average (+140.8%) remains visibly divorced from its median right in the same row - exactly the discrepancy the disclaimer/note now calls out; all 11 signal types' live table values matched an independent manual recalculation done during the investigation. Errors or unresolved items: previously-saved snapshot #1 (from before this fix) still reflects the old pre-fix numbers/shape, which is expected and correct per the append-only/frozen-snapshot design - it simply now differs from the live report, which itself is the point of a dated snapshot. Next step: none planned.
2026-08-11 - Added a "signal quality" summary layer to the Patterns page so the takeaways I'd been giving in chat are visible on the page itself, per user's explicit request ("the UI does not reflect that. how do you propose we add that signal quality?" -> "do it"). Files changed: src/db/patterns.ts (SignalPatternGroup gained `nDistinctTokens`; Comparison now carries the token address and classifyComparison/summarizeGroup compute distinct-token counts over the "fresh" subset specifically, i.e. the same population up%/avg/median are computed from), tests/patterns.test.ts (new test: two signals sharing one token plus one signal on a different token must report nFresh=3 but nDistinctTokens=2, not 3), ui/main.tsx (new `patternVerdict` derived value that auto-generates a one-line plain-language summary per horizon from the already-computed groups - e.g. which types have a positive median vs how many are net-negative - reusing the existing reliable flag so small-sample groups can't drive the headline claim; three new headline stat tiles above the table reusing the existing `.quality-grid`/`.quality-metric` styling already used on the Snapshot Analysis page: overall genuine-outcome coverage %, overall up % at the selected horizon, and overall median return; new "tokens" column in the pattern table with a distinct highlight/tooltip when a group's distinct-token count is lower than its fresh comparison count, meaning the same token repeated and the comparisons aren't fully independent), ui/styles.css (`.pattern-repeat-tokens` highlight style). Decision made and reason: computed nDistinctTokens over the "fresh" subset rather than all-n, since that's the exact population the up%/avg/median stats are drawn from, so the token-repeat caveat lines up with what those specific numbers mean rather than a broader, less precise count; reused existing tile/note CSS classes instead of inventing new ones to keep the page visually consistent with the rest of the app. Agent name and model: Claude Sonnet 5. Test result: `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx tsc -p tsconfig.ui.json --noEmit` both clean; `npm run build` clean; `npm test` 60/60 passing (59 prior + 1 new). Verified live against the real running dev server and real database: headline tiles read "62% signals with a genuine outcome (414 of 670)", "25.4% up overall at +1h", "-39.4% overall median return"; auto-verdict read "At +1h: 7 · Price ATH is the only type with a positive median return; 10 other types with enough data are net-negative" - matching exactly what was said in chat; type 12 "Smart-money buy" row shows fresh=49 but tokens=38 with the repeat-token highlight and tooltip active (confirming some tokens fired that signal type more than once); type 7 shows fresh=18/tokens=13, also flagged. Errors or unresolved items: none. Next step: none planned.
2026-08-11 - Widened the GMGN browser-capture extension to watch more endpoints, after live-inspecting network traffic on a real gmgn.ai token page (https://gmgn.ai/sol/token/4LHBp4xYs3suKjkCuo1qaNaxufWd4Bdp6QYsBJZNpump, navigated read-only via the browser tool) to see what else was available beyond the existing `/vas/api/v1/token-signal` target. Found and added: `/api/v1/token_mcap_candles/` (OHLC price/mcap candles - a cleaner potential price source than reconstructing prices from raw Dune trades), `/vas/api/v1/token_trades/` (recent raw trades from GMGN's own indexer), `/vas/api/v1/token_holder_stat/` (holder count/concentration), `/defi/quotation/v1/smartmoney/sol/wallet/` (per-wallet smart-money reputation, ties to the `smart_degen_wallets` field already present in captured signal payloads). Deliberately did not add `/api/v1/token_stat/` (likely redundant with fields already in the signal payload) or telemetry/UI-state endpoints (Sentry envelope, cdn-cgi/rum beacon, /account/iploc, gas_price_list, wallet-api/get_coins). Also noted but did not implement: `token-signal` did not fire during this observed page load despite the URL's `utm_source=signal_newpop` implying it was reached via a live signal push - the "new signal" event may be WebSocket-delivered, which the extension's current fetch/XHR-patching approach (content-main.js) would not see; this project has no existing WebSocket capture code, so if live signal delivery is in fact WS-based this is an unaddressed gap, not something already covered. Files changed: extension/content-main.js (TARGET_PATH -> TARGET_PATHS array, matched via .some/.includes so the existing dynamic-segment substring-matching behavior is unchanged for each new prefix), extension/manifest.json (version 0.2.0 -> 0.3.0, description updated; host_permissions already covered gmgn.ai so no permission change needed), extension/popup.html (description text updated). **Also fixed a real ingestion-corruption risk this change would otherwise have introduced**: src/gmgn/browserImport.ts's importer had no requestPath filter at all - it blindly ran every capture's response body through storeGmgnSignal regardless of endpoint, which was harmless only because the extension previously ever captured one endpoint shape. Widening capture without this fix would have pushed candle/trade/holder-stat/wallet payloads through signal parsing, generating garbage rows with mostly-null fields in gmgn_signals. Added a `SIGNAL_REQUEST_PATH` constant and gated the storeGmgnSignal loop so only captures whose requestPath matches the real token-signal endpoint are parsed as signals; all other captures are now counted separately as `otherCaptures` (new field on BrowserImportResult and in the archived manifest.json) and preserved in the raw archived export, but never parsed - a deliberate placeholder until dedicated importers for the new endpoint types are built, not an oversight. Also fixed tests/gmgn-browser-import.test.ts's fixture, which used a stale placeholder requestPath (`/v1/market/token_signal`) that never matched the extension's real path (`/vas/api/v1/token-signal`) - harmless before this change since nothing checked requestPath, but would have broken every existing test once the filter was added; updated to the real path. Files changed: src/gmgn/browserImport.ts, tests/gmgn-browser-import.test.ts (fixed fixture path, +1 test proving candle/trade/holder/wallet captures are counted but never stored as signals, including on the duplicate-file fast path), ui/main.tsx (BrowserImportResult type + status line show otherCaptures). Agent name and model: Claude Sonnet 5. Test result: `node --check` clean on all four extension scripts, manifest JSON parses; `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx tsc -p tsconfig.ui.json --noEmit` both clean; `npm run build` clean; `npm test` 61/61 passing (60 prior + 1 new). Errors or unresolved items: the unpacked extension has not been reloaded/tested in real Chrome yet (no extension-loading capability in this environment) - user needs to reload it in chrome://extensions and verify a real capture picks up the new endpoint types; whether GMGN's live "new signal" push is WebSocket-based remains unconfirmed; no dedicated importer/table exists yet for the four new endpoint types, so their captured data is archived raw but not yet queryable structured data. 2026-08-13 - Added WebSocket capture to test mode, per explicit user request, to see the GMGN Signal feed's live push traffic that fetch/XHR patching cannot observe. Files changed: extension/content-main.js (patched `window.WebSocket` the same way fetch/XHR already are - wraps the constructor, returns the real socket instance untouched via `new`'s explicit-return semantics so `instanceof`/readyState/static constants all stay correct, and unconditionally attaches a `message` listener to any same-host-as-gmgn.ai socket; each incoming message is gated by the same `Date.now() < testModeUntil` check used everywhere else in test mode, so nothing is recorded unless a human has an active discovery window open. String messages are JSON-parsed with a raw-text fallback; Blob messages are read via `.text()` then treated the same way; binary ArrayBuffer frames are deliberately left uncaptured since every GMGN endpoint observed so far has been JSON-over-text), extension/manifest.json (version 0.4.0 -> 0.5.0, description updated; no new permissions needed - observing a WebSocket the page itself already opened doesn't require host_permissions the way an extension-initiated request would), extension/popup.html (small-print description updated to mention WebSocket messages). Decision made and reason: unlike TARGET_PATHS, there is no known-good permanent filter yet for this feed (URL, message shape, and even whether it's JSON are all unconfirmed), so WebSocket capture is scoped to test mode only, to be narrowed into a permanent, precise capture once a real discovery run reveals the actual shape - mirrors the same "discover broadly in test mode, then promote to permanent" workflow already used for the four HTTP endpoints added earlier. Deliberately attached the message listener unconditionally rather than gating on `testModeUntil` at connection time: GMGN's socket almost certainly connects once on page load, so gating attachment on test-mode-already-active would mean a connection made before clicking "Start test mode" could never be observed without a fresh page reload after starting - since `content-main.js` runs at `document_start` (before any page script), the patched constructor is guaranteed to be in place for the very first WebSocket the page ever creates regardless of when test mode is later toggled, so this ordering issue is fully closed for the constructor patch itself; only the per-message recording is time-gated, not the listener attachment. Agent name and model: Claude Sonnet 5. Test result: `node --check` clean on content-main.js; `npm test` 61/61 passing (no backend files touched, sanity-checked only). Next step: user runs a fresh test-mode capture on the Trenches/Signal feed page and shares the export; if `ws-connect:` entries appear but no `ws-message:` ones, that would point to either a binary protocol or a connection to a different host `sameGmgnHost` doesn't match, both worth checking before concluding WebSocket capture didn't work.

2026-08-13 - Verified permanent WS signal capture end-to-end against real Chrome for the first time, then found and fixed a real background.js bug that could silently kill capture entirely. Verification: analyzed a real normal-capture export the user shared (gmgn-browser-capture-2026-08-13T05-10-54-690Z.json, extensionVersion 0.6.0): 22 real `ws-message:` captures over ~28 seconds, correctly filtered `token_signal` channel data, alongside the existing HTTP-endpoint captures - confirms the prior entry's extension change genuinely works live, not just in theory. Bug found: user reported "nothing is being captured" and shared chrome://extensions' error log showing `Uncaught (in promise) Error: Resource::kQuotaBytes quota exceeded` inside `background.js`'s heartbeat/capture-storage code, plus `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'active')` and `Extension context invalidated` in the popup. Root cause: `handleCapture`/`handleTestCapture` were fire-and-forget (`chrome.runtime.onMessage` calls them without await or .catch), and their `chrome.storage.local.set(...)` call had no try/catch at all - so once a write ever failed (quota or any other transient storage error), it became an invisible unhandled promise rejection, the newly-pushed capture was discarded, and every subsequent capture attempt would fail identically and silently forever, since nothing ever shrank the stored array or retried. Separately, popup.js's message calls had no error handling either, so a popup left open across an extension reload ("Extension context invalidated") threw trying to read a field off the rejected call's result. Files changed: extension/background.js (new shared `appendToStorage(key, item)` helper: on a failed `chrome.storage.local.set`, logs via `console.error`, trims the oldest half of the array, and retries once before giving up on that single item - both `handleCapture` and `handleTestCapture` now go through it instead of duplicating the raw get/push/set), extension/popup.js (new `safeSendMessage` wrapper around every `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` call; on failure, shows "Extension was reloaded — close and reopen this popup." instead of crashing), extension/manifest.json (version 0.6.0 -> 0.6.1, patch only, no permission/description change). Decision made and reason: chose reactive trim-on-failure (not a proactive cap on every write) so normal long capture sessions never lose data unless a write is actually failing - only pay the trimming cost when there's a real problem. Did not investigate why quota was hit despite the `unlimitedStorage` permission already being in the manifest since 0.4.0 (possible causes not yet ruled out: an unreloaded pre-0.4.0 instance, or a genuine disk-quota edge case) - the defensive fix makes this non-fatal regardless of root cause, which was the priority given capture was actively broken for the user. Agent name and model: Claude Sonnet 5. Test result: `node --check` clean on background.js and popup.js; manifest JSON parses; `npm test` 62/62 passing (extension-only change, backend unaffected, sanity-checked). Errors or unresolved items: not yet re-verified live in Chrome after this fix (no extension-loading capability in this environment); root cause of the original quota hit (given unlimitedStorage should prevent it) remains unconfirmed - if it recurs even after clearing captured data and reloading to 0.6.1, that would point to something other than plain accumulated volume. Next step: user reloads the unpacked extension to 0.6.1, clears both captured-data stores from the popup to clear the current backlog, confirms the popup no longer shows a blank/broken state, and resumes capture; if the quota error recurs, capture the exact chrome://extensions error log again for further diagnosis.

91 2026-08-13 - Implemented the reconciliation workflow flagged as missing in the prior entry, plus fixed a related in-loop bug: a single timed-out/failed batch inside "Measure eligible" was aborting every batch after it, not just the failing one. Investigated first (read-only) at the user's request ("currently it stops when facing a time out, will your reconciliation help skip the stuck call and move on to next call?") and confirmed these are two separate problems needing two separate fixes, not one: reconciliation frees signals stuck from *past* runs, but does nothing for a batch failing *mid-run* since the old code wrapped the entire batch loop in one try/catch that aborted on the first error. Files changed: src/dune/outcomes.ts (new `reconcileDuneRun(database, runId)` - a single, non-blocking status poll against a run's stored `execution_id`, never re-submitting the query so it can't create a duplicate execution; finalizes to `completed` (archives + merges into the same read path as a fresh measurement) or `failed` (Dune-side failure/cancellation, or a `submitted` run that never got an execution_id and is older than a 10-minute grace period), otherwise leaves it untouched and reports `still_running`; new `reconcileStuckDuneRuns(database)` sweeps every `submitted`/`running`/`timed_out` run once and tallies outcomes), src/scripts/server.ts (new `POST /api/dune/reconcile` route), ui/main.tsx (new `reconcileStuckRuns()` action wired to a "Reconcile N in-flight signals" button that only renders when `inFlight > 0`; `measureAllOutcomes` now calls reconciliation once up front so previously-stuck signals are eligible before batching; the batch loop's try/catch moved from wrapping the whole loop to wrapping each batch individually, so one failing/timed-out batch is recorded in the final message and the loop continues to the next batch instead of stopping). 2026-08-14 - Reviewed the Patterns page logic against live SQLite-backed API results; no code changed. Current data: 11,662 captured signals, all with at least one stored outcome; 5,969 complete, 5,449 retry-eligible, 72 pending, 169 retry-delayed, 3 retry-exhausted. Across fresh comparisons the overall median is negative at every populated horizon (-16.3% at +5m, -29.8% at +15m, -36.7% at +30m, -39.9% at +1h, -44.2% at +3h); +6h/+24h/+7d have no fresh data. Only type 5 at +5m is positive under the existing reliability gate (median +1.5%, up 62.1%, 29 tokens/3 dates/63% coverage), but worst return is -92% and maximum baseline trade age is ~10 hours, so it is a hypothesis rather than a validated winner. Positive subgroup cells exist (notably type 8 + Pump.fun + age 1-24h and type 12 + Pump.fun + age 1-24h), but 98 combined cells are tested with no multiple-testing correction or holdout validation. Code review findings: coverage denominators and capture-date gates are calculated before first-token/type dedup, so repeats can understate coverage while unrelated matured/repeated dates can overstate date diversity; trade-age is diagnostic only and extreme return outliers remain; best-horizon UI selection requires only nFresh >= 10 rather than full reliability. Decision: do not treat current Patterns verdicts as production winner rules. Tests: read-only API inspection; no test run needed. Next step: correct analysis-unit denominators/date counting, enforce a preregistered trade-age policy, add robust/outlier diagnostics and held-out validation, then promote only stable rules.
2026-08-14 - Investigated reported silent loading during initial page load and Measure outcomes, without changing code. Findings: initial `refresh()` fires 12 API requests in parallel but has no refresh-busy state or loading shell; `measurement-plan` synchronously scans all signals/outcomes, runs the pre-screen, and writes decisions for every candidate before returning. Direct local timing observed `/api/dune/measurement-plan` at ~29.75s with a 2.4 MB response, `/api/dune/candidates` at ~20.61s with a 2.0 MB response, and `/api/analysis/patterns` at ~26.59s. Because the Node server handles these synchronous database operations on the request path, parallel browser calls can queue and make the page look stalled. On Measure, `measureAllOutcomes()` first calls reconciliation and refreshes the measurement plan before setting `outcomeBatchBusy`, so the visible busy state starts late; each Dune batch can then block up to 60 seconds while polling Dune. Batch progress exists only after preflight begins, as `{completed}/{total}` and batch number; no progress is exposed for initial refresh, pre-screen computation, reconciliation, or the preflight plan refresh. No code changed. Agent/model: Codex GPT-5. Tests: read-only timing checks only; no test run required. Next step: design a global operation-status model with an initial-load spinner, preflight status, and batch progress; only show percentages where totals are known.

Bug 1 (confirmed by direct reproduction): browserImport.ts's raw-endpoint routing reads chain/period/type/window/orderby/has_token from `captureUrl(capture).searchParams`, but real captures never carry a query string in requestPath - confirmed directly against the real downloaded file (`gmgn-browser-capture-2026-08-14T05-20-44-554Z.json`)'s actual capture object keys (capturedAt/requestPath/responseBody/status only, requestPath already query-string-stripped per extension/content-main.js's own comment) and by importing a realistically-shaped fixture through the actual importGmgnBrowserCapture function: radar's chain/period/category all stored as null, wallet-rank's orderby null (window masked by a hardcoded '?? 7d' fallback so the bug isn't even visible), and twitter's has_token always null - defeating the entire reason that endpoint was worth capturing (distinguishing token-linked tweets from general chatter). The existing tests pass only because their fixtures invent a requestPath with a query string appended, which doesn't match reality - exactly the "verify against real data, don't invent fixtures" mistake this project's conventions otherwise consistently avoid. Root cause: the migration that created this table originally had `... UNIQUE` (matching the other three raw-endpoint tables) before a different agent "corrected" it to plain `NOT NULL` for append-only semantics (progress.md entry 101) - but they edited the already-applied migration's SQL text in place instead of adding a new migration, which has zero effect on a database where it already ran (migrations here are tracked by count, not re-diffed). The real database was silently still carrying the old UNIQUE constraint, so every second capture of an unchanged wallet (very common for low-activity wallets - identical raw_payload produces an identical hash) threw an uncaught constraint violation, silently recorded as an "error"/issue rather than the append-only observation it was supposed to be. Fixed via the standard SQLite rebuild (rename, recreate without the constraint, copy rows, drop old, recreate the lookup index) since SQLite has no ALTER TABLE DROP CONSTRAINT.
- src/gmgn/rawEndpointReads.ts (new): `listRadarSnapshots`/`listWalletRankSnapshots`/`listSmartMoneyWalletStats` (optionally wallet-filtered)/`listTwitterMessages` (most-recent-first, parsed raw_payload, capped limit) plus `readRawEndpointSummary` (per-type count + latest captured_at).

109 2026-08-14 - Reviewed a performance/UX investigation report (logged by a different agent/session) claiming `/api/dune/measurement-plan` (~30s), `/api/dune/candidates` (~21s), and `/api/analysis/patterns` (~27s) were each independently expensive and recommending new staged-progress UI. Verified the numbers directly rather than trusting them: measured alone, candidates was 0.08s and patterns 0.47s - nowhere near the reported figures - but firing all three concurrently (matching the real page-load `refresh()` pattern) reproduced ~50s for all three simultaneously. Root-caused this precisely: Node's single-threaded event loop was fully blocked by `runPrescreen`'s decision-writing loop in src/dune/prescreen.ts, which called `database.prepare(...).run(...)` once per candidate (~11,700 in production) with no transaction wrapper, so every write auto-committed to disk individually; candidates/patterns were not slow themselves, they were queued behind this one blocking call. Reproduced the cost in isolation on a synthetic production-scale dataset (11,662 rows, real on-disk SQLite file, not :memory:): 27.1s cold, 27.4s warm/idempotent re-run - proving the cost was 100% the write pattern, not the pre-screen decision logic (which is fast, plain JS). Confirmed the fix before applying it to production code: the same 11,662 writes wrapped in one transaction measured 49ms (~550x). Checked for concurrent-edit conflicts first (per explicit user request "review latest changes first") since prescreen.ts/planner.ts/main.tsx had all been touched very recently by a different session - confirmed the concurrent changes (new `selectedNewCount`/`selectedRetryCount`/`statePriority` fields, prioritizing never-measured rows over retries) were unrelated to the write loop and didn't conflict. Files changed: src/dune/prescreen.ts (hoisted the prepared statement out of the loop, wrapped the whole decision-write loop in BEGIN/COMMIT with a ROLLBACK-and-rethrow on error, added a comment citing the measured before/after numbers so the reasoning survives future edits). Decision made and reason: fixed the actual root cause instead of building the report's proposed remediation (multi-stage progress indicators, percentage bars for pre-screening/reconciliation/DB scans), since that infrastructure becomes largely unnecessary once the real bug is fixed - a sub-second page load doesn't need a staged-loading UI; the report's remaining valid UX points (Measure button busy-state timing, per-batch Dune poll percentage) are real but far lower priority against a 550x backend win. 2026-08-14 - Applied the 24h observation buffer retroactively to old, already-measured signals stuck by pre-rule premature retries, per explicit user request ("do not ignore past data, same threshold should apply to them too"). Confirmed live before implementing: 11,653 of 11,662 measured signals had a first Dune attempt fired before the signal was 24h old (expected, since almost all history predates the buffer), but only 28 of those are actually stuck (permanently retry_exhausted with unresolved checkpoints) - the rest are already complete with real received data, which is valid regardless of query timing, so a blanket re-measure of all history was explicitly avoided as wasteful. Files changed: src/dune/planner.ts (MEASUREMENT_PLAN_VERSION bumped v5->v6; signalIdsWithRuns now takes an observedAtById map and excludes any completed run whose requested_at fired before the signal's own observed_at + MIN_SIGNAL_AGE_MS from the attempts/lastCompletedAt count used for retry-exhaustion scheduling; computeMeasurementPlan builds and passes that map). Decision and reason: discount premature attempts from the retry budget instead of resetting/deleting them, so signals stuck by pre-rule attempts become retry_eligible again and get a genuine post-24h shot, while every future attempt (including all first attempts, gated by too_fresh) naturally fires post-24h and still counts normally toward exhaustion - bounded, cannot be exploited into infinite retries. Tests added: tests/dune-planner.test.ts (+2: a signal with 4 premature completed attempts is retry_eligible not retry_exhausted; a signal with 4 attempts fired at/after the buffer still properly exhausts), version assertion updated to v6. Every finding was verified against the live .data/crypto-research.sqlite rather than inferred from reading. Also recorded: prescreen labels 6,744 premature-only signals "already_measured"; the pre-screen panel explains deferred_budget via a 500-request cap the retry path bypasses entirely; patterns derives its horizon list from stored data while planner uses the CHECKPOINT_LABELS constant, so 25 legacy signals produce 3 phantom +6h/+24h/+7d horizons; a provably dead clause in readAllDuneOutcomes' merge condition (verified 0 of 16 status combinations where it changes the result); an unreachable '>24h' fallback naming a bucket label that no longer exists; eligibleRetrySignalIds shipped in every payload but never read by the UI; MIN_RELIABLE_SAMPLE duplicated in robustPatterns.ts; and a literal NUL byte in patterns.ts/robustPatterns.ts dedup keys that makes git and ripgrep treat both analysis files as binary. Each sell carries buy_cost_usd, so per-trade return is directly computable, and every row has tx_hash for later independent Dune verification.

Trade history needs GMGN_API_KEY only - GMGN_PRIVATE_KEY (the trading key) is not required and must stay unconfigured.

Backend owns src/ + tests/ + schema; frontend owns ui/main.tsx + ui/styles.css only. Dune verification is deliberately deferred to a later step - the schema is designed to accept it, but building it now would widen the first slice too far. Both prompts carry this repo's paid-for lessons forward: median over average, tx_hash is not a unique row key, no premature measurement, declarative sort handlers, and verify the new UI is not hidden by the focused-view CSS.

2026-08-15 - Implemented the CopyTrade backend from research/prompts/copytrade-backend-task.md. Frontend remains unbuilt (separate prompt, not started).
- Step: Built fetch -> persist -> evaluate for top-trader trade history, using the official GMGN API rather than scraping.
- Files added: src/copytrade/roster.ts (leaderboard snapshot -> wallet roster, wash_trader risk flags), src/copytrade/fetch.ts (cursor-paged gmgn-cli portfolio activity, append-only persistence, run-state tracking), src/copytrade/evaluate.ts (per-trade returns, median/average, $100 outcome, four-gate verdict), tests/copytrade.test.ts (16 tests).
- Files changed: src/db/schema.ts (migration 16: copytrade_wallets, copytrade_trades, copytrade_fetch_runs, copytrade_result_snapshots), src/scripts/server.ts (five routes + startup reconcile), tests/schema.test.ts (table list).
- Decisions: money values stored as TEXT verbatim because GMGN returns high-precision decimal strings that REAL would silently round. dedup_key is wallet|tx_hash|token|event|amount|timestamp, not tx_hash alone, because one Solana transaction can contain several DEX legs - the same mistake already fixed once in the Dune ranking SQL. A sell is the unit of analysis, using GMGN's own buy_cost_usd as cost basis; sells with no cost basis (tokens transferred in, not bought) are excluded and counted, never treated as 0%. Verdict gates: 100+ trades, 7+ days, positive median, no risk flag; risk outranks a thin sample because it is a claim about whether the numbers can be trusted at all. Trade history needs GMGN_API_KEY only - GMGN_PRIVATE_KEY is never read or passed, so this path is structurally incapable of moving funds.
- Two real bugs found during live verification, not by reading code:
  (1) The first live run hung permanently at request 103 with requests_made and trades_fetched both frozen and the 30s execFile timeout never firing. execFile only settles once the child has exited AND its stdio pipes close, so a child that exits while something holds its stdout open leaves the promise pending forever - freezing the run with no error and no recovery, and latching the 409 single-run guard so no future fetch could start. Fixed with an explicit Promise timer that kills the child and settles regardless of pipe state, plus a cursor-repeat guard, a three-consecutive-barren-page guard, requests_made now incremented before the request so a stall is distinguishable from inactivity, and reconcileStaleFetchRuns() at startup (a fetch only runs in the process that started it, so any row still 'running' at startup is orphaned by definition). After the fix the same 3-wallet fetch completed in under 15 seconds.
  (2) Sequential full-bankroll compounding produced $1.35e+22 for a wallet whose median trade LOST 0.67%. Not a display bug - the model is wrong for these traders: it assumes one position at a time while a memecoin trader runs many concurrently, so a +5000% return on one small position is credited as a 50x on the entire bankroll. Headline endingCapitalUsd is now an equal-weight portfolio ($100 split across every trade; the mean is the correct aggregator for a portfolio, while the median answers what a typical trade did). The compounded figure is retained as endingCapitalUsdCompounded because the gap between the two is itself the signal that returns are outlier-dominated; non-finite results return null rather than Infinity.

2026-08-15 - Added a stop control and closed the four coverage/correctness gaps found by reasoning through the fetch loop's behavior across changed parameters.
- Step: Implemented stop, a per-wallet request cap with truncation recorded, watermark-aware paging, truncation-aware reporting, and roster scoping.
- Files changed: src/db/schema.ts (migration 17: copytrade_wallet_coverage), src/copytrade/fetch.ts, src/copytrade/roster.ts, src/copytrade/evaluate.ts, src/scripts/server.ts (POST /api/copytrade/fetch/stop, ?limit= on results), tests/copytrade.test.ts (+6), tests/schema.test.ts, research/prompts/copytrade-frontend-task.md (contract updated for the parallel frontend agent).
- Gap 1, extending the period silently backfilled nothing. Paging is newest-first and cannot skip, so extending 30d -> 60d must re-walk known history to reach older data - but every one of those pages is a duplicate, which tripped the three-barren-page stalled-cursor guard within seconds and reported 'completed'. Fixed by deriving a per-wallet watermark (MIN/MAX observed_timestamp, derived not stored, so it cannot drift from the trades actually held) and suppressing barren counting while inside known coverage; the guard now only fires outside it, which is the only place a stalled cursor is distinguishable from an expected duplicate stretch. A re-run of an already-covered window now also stops at the newest watermark ('up_to_date') instead of walking the whole history.
- Gap 2, no cap. MAX_PAGES_PER_WALLET was 2000 (~100,000 trades, ~7 minutes), so one high-volume wallet could consume an entire run. Replaced with MAX_REQUESTS_PER_WALLET = 200, sized from what the statistics need rather than what the API can deliver: median and win rate are a quantile and a proportion, both well determined by roughly 2,000 completed sells. Capped wallets are recorded truncated with a stop_reason.
- Gap 3, truncation was invisible. Capping keeps the newest N trades, so the sample is a recent slice, not a random draw - which biases the mean but not the median or win rate. Truncated wallets now return null for averageReturnPercent, endingCapitalUsd and endingCapitalUsdCompounded (withheld, not zeroed) plus unreliableReason, coveredDays and needsDuneBackfill; the pooled overall row is suppressed the same way when any truncated wallet contributes.
- Gap 4, reducing the trader count did not shrink the report, because it read the trades table directly and ignored the roster. Results now accept ?limit= and scope to the current roster. Related: listRosterWallets took the newest row per wallet across all snapshots, so wallets that had dropped off the leaderboard stayed in the roster and two wallets could each claim rank_position 1 from different snapshots, making 'top N' ill-defined; it is now scoped to the newest snapshot, with allSnapshots opt-in for history.
- Decision: cancellation state is in-memory and discharged in a finally block - a run only executes in the process that started it, and leaving a stale id set could silently abort a future run that reused it.
- Agent/model: Claude Opus 5.
- Tests: npx tsc -b --noEmit clean; npm run build clean; npm test 155/155 passing (149 prior + 6 new).
- Live verification against the real API and database (migration applied, user_version 16 -> 17): (1) re-running the same period used 1 request per wallet and stopped 'up_to_date', against 3-4 before; (2) extending 1d -> 3d genuinely backfilled - wallet 4uCT went from 2,250 trades/47.9h to 3,150 trades/69.5h in 63 requests, stop_reason 'window_covered', where the old code would have aborted after three duplicate pages; (3) stop halted an in-flight run in 1 second with status 'cancelled', 4,900 trades kept and the interrupted wallet's coverage row recording 137 requests, and stop with nothing running returned {stopped:false} cleanly; (4) the cap fired at exactly 200 requests with truncated=1 and stop_reason 'request_cap'; (5) the truncated wallet rendered median -9.81% with average and $100 as nulls plus both unreliableReason strings, and ?limit=1/2/3 returned 1/2/3 rows against 7 unscoped.
- Errors/unresolved: my earlier estimate of ~40s per capped wallet was wrong - measured ~144s (200 requests at 0.2s spacing plus ~0.5s per CLI call), so 100 capped wallets is roughly 4 hours, not 1. Still far better than the 11-hour worst case, but the frontend should not promise an hour. Dune backfill for truncated wallets is NOT built: it needs the trader column name in dex_solana.trades verified by one test query first, and guessing it would be worse than leaving the hook. needsDuneBackfill marks the wallets that want it. No wallet has yet returned verdict 'yes' - several now clear the trade/day/median gates (Daumen median +13.85% over 29 days, Zrool +3.4%) but every one carries the wash_trader flag.
- Next: build the frontend from the updated prompt; separately, verify the Dune trader column and build the backfill path for the wallets flagged needsDuneBackfill.

2026-08-15 - Made the reason behind a risk flag visible and measurable, instead of passing through an opaque vendor tag.
- Step: Added per-wallet risk evidence to the CopyTrade report.
- Files changed: src/db/schema.ts (migration 18: copytrade_wallet_stats), src/copytrade/fetch.ts (fetchAndStoreWalletStats via the official portfolio stats endpoint, one extra weight-3 call per wallet, wrapped so a failure never aborts the trade fetch), src/copytrade/evaluate.ts (holdSecondsPerSell, RiskEvidence, buildRiskNotes, riskEvidence/riskNotes on every row), tests/copytrade.test.ts (+3), tests/schema.test.ts.
- Finding that shaped the design: GMGN's "Phishing check" block (no_buy_hold, fast_tx, sell_pass_buy, token_honeypot) is NOT in the official API - verified live against a real wallet, `portfolio stats` returns only pnl_stat and common. Those numbers exist only on the browser endpoint /pf/api/v1/wallet/{chain}/{addr}/profit_stat/{period}. So rather than depend on a browser-only source, the quantitative evidence is now computed from our own stored trades: fastRoundTripPercent (sells closing within 60s of the wallet's last buy of that token), noCostBasisPercent (sells of tokens never bought), and medianHoldSeconds. The official endpoint is still called, for the surrounding context it does give: funding origin, wallet age, avg holding period, token count.
- Decision: evidence is presented as observations, never as proof. The note set opens by stating GMGN does not publish its wash_trader criteria, and every measurement is reproducible from raw payloads. Fast round trips and received-not-bought tokens each have innocent explanations (scalping, airdrops), so they are stated, not concluded from.
- Agent/model: Claude Opus 5.
- Tests: npx tsc -b --noEmit clean; npm run build clean; npm test 158/158 passing (155 prior + 3 new).
- Live verification (migration applied, user_version 17 -> 18; stats fetched for 7 wallets): every row now carries measured evidence. Fixed a real display defect found only by looking at live output - hold time rendered as "0 minutes" for every wallet because Math.round of a sub-30-second median is 0; it now reports seconds below a minute, which is exactly the range these wallets occupy.
- IMPORTANT NEGATIVE RESULT: the computed evidence does not reproduce GMGN's tag and must not be presented as if it does. Across the 7 fetched wallets, fast round trips run 59.5-93% and no-cost-basis sells 6.9-13.6%, but the two UNFLAGGED wallets (decu 91%, theo 79%) score higher on fast round trips than three of the five flagged ones (Daumen 59.5%, Cupsey 75.5%, Zrool 78.8%). Median hold is 8-40 seconds for every wallet regardless of flag. So either GMGN uses criteria we cannot see, or sub-minute round-tripping is simply the norm for this whole cohort and does not discriminate at all. The evidence is honest and useful context; it is not a wash-trade detector, and no code treats it as one - the verdict still keys off the GMGN tag alone.
- Also observed: 4 of 7 wallets were first funded from AxiomRXZAq1Jgjj9pHmNqVP7Lhu67wLXZJZbaK87TTSk. That is the Axiom trading-bot platform's deposit address, so it indicates "operated through Axiom", not "same operator" - worth stating carefully rather than as a sybil finding.
- Errors/unresolved: FAST_ROUND_TRIP_SECONDS = 60 is an unvalidated threshold picked for readability, not derived from data; the 10% reporting floor in buildRiskNotes is likewise arbitrary. Hold time is measured against the wallet's most recent prior buy of the same token, not a FIFO lot match, so it is approximate for partially-closed positions. Dune backfill for truncated wallets remains unbuilt pending verification of the trader column in dex_solana.trades.
- Next: build the frontend; the contract gained riskEvidence and riskNotes on every row (both additive).
2026-08-15 - Implemented the P0 set from an external review (items 1, 2, 3, 7) plus three defects the review did not catch. All four review claims were verified empirically before any code changed, not accepted on reading.
- Files changed: src/copytrade/evaluate.ts, src/copytrade/roster.ts, src/copytrade/fetch.ts, src/scripts/server.ts, ui/main.tsx, tests/copytrade.test.ts (+5 regression tests).
- Review item 1 (results not scoped to roster): CONFIRMED, and worse than reported. Scoping only applied when traderLimit was supplied, so the default report - which is exactly what the UI requested - included wallets that had dropped off the leaderboard, AND rendered them with an EMPTY risk-flag list because their roster entry no longer resolved. A wallet GMGN had tagged wash_trader therefore displayed as clean. Fixed: roster membership is now unconditional, with no opt-out.
- Review item 3 (span from unusable sells): CONFIRMED, and materially worse. Reproduced: 120 usable trades inside five minutes plus one cost-basis-less sell from 20 days earlier produced coveredDays 19.96 and verdict `yes` with zero failed rules. That would have been the first positive verdict this system ever produced, and it was spurious. Fixed by deriving span solely from entry.completed.
- Review item 7 (truncation not a gate): CONFIRMED. decideVerdict had no truncation input, so a capped wallet could earn `yes` while its average was simultaneously withheld as untrustworthy in the same row. Added a `truncated` gate and a new `incomplete` verdict, ranked between flagged and thin - incomplete says the sample is not the one requested, which more of the same data would not fix, so collapsing it into `thin` would have misdescribed it.
- Review item 2 (snapshots ignore displayed params): CONFIRMED. The route called computeCopyTradeReport() with no arguments. Fixed by adding a self-describing `scope` block to the report (chain, traderLimit, rosterSnapshotId, rosterSize, methodologyVersion) and having the snapshot read params off the report rather than recomputing defaults. METHODOLOGY_VERSION introduced as copytrade-evaluation-v2 so a frozen snapshot can never be silently compared against a report computed under different rules.
- Three defects the review missed, all fixed: (a) storeActivityPage's single `skipped` counter conflated duplicates (the normal outcome of re-fetching an overlapping window) with malformed rows (actual data loss) - now counted separately; (b) entry.first/last were seeded from whichever row appeared first including a buy, inflating span independently of the excluded-sell bug; (c) unparseable risk_flags JSON returned an empty array, i.e. an unknown risk state presented as clean and eligible for a positive verdict - now returns an explicit UNKNOWN_RISK_FLAG marker that trips every risk gate.
- Frontend: /api/copytrade/results is now called with the selected periodDays and limit (previously neither, so the controls scoped only the next fetch, not the report); the snapshot POST sends the same params; the verdict label/icon maps gained `incomplete` (they are Record-typed, so a new verdict would otherwise have rendered blank).
- Agent/model: Claude Opus 5.
- Tests: npx tsc -b --noEmit and tsc -p tsconfig.ui.json --noEmit both clean; npm run build clean; npm test 163/163 passing (158 prior + 5 regression tests, one per fix).

POST snapshot with {periodDays:7, limit:3} stored params_json with periodDays 7, traderLimit 3, rosterSize 3 - previously it would have frozen the 30-day all-wallet default.
- Errors/unresolved: review items 4 (hindsight/survivorship), 5 (raw page + malformed row preservation), 6 (append-only coverage history), and 8 (trade-weighted vs wallet-weighted overall) are NOT implemented. On item 5 I would push back on scope: the malformed-row half is cheap and clearly right, but "store every fetched page unchanged" is roughly 1GB per full run against an 820MB database, and the repo's existing ZIP+SHA-256 archive pattern is the better fit than a SQLite table.

2026-08-15 - Implemented review item 8 (trade-weighted vs wallet-weighted overall). Item 6 was handed to a parallel agent.
- Files changed: src/copytrade/evaluate.ts, ui/main.tsx, tests/copytrade.test.ts (+2).
- Decision: the existing pooled row is now explicitly labelled `trade-weighted` and a second `overallByWallet` row is returned alongside it, never instead of it. Pooling every trade lets the busiest wallet define the headline - in the live data one wallet holds 6,370 of 15,632 trades - so it answers "across all this activity, what happened?" while wallet-weighting answers "how does a typical trader on this list do?". Both are true; reporting only the first was the defect. The wallet-weighted median is a median of per-wallet medians, not of pooled trades. Truncated wallets are excluded from the wallet-weighted averages rather than counted as zero, matching the rule used everywhere else here.
- Contract: overall gained `weighting` and `wallets`; the report gained `overallByWallet`. Both additive. The UI now renders two summary rows, labelled "by trade" and "by wallet".
- Parallel split: items 6 and 8 have zero file overlap (6 is schema.ts + fetch.ts, 8 is evaluate.ts + ui/main.tsx), so they were run concurrently. Item 4 touches all four files plus the UI and must follow alone - it was NOT started. Prompt written at research/prompts/copytrade-coverage-history-task.md, which explicitly fences the other agent out of evaluate.ts and ui/main.tsx and tells it to append-only in the shared test file.

- Finding: the app does not independently apply a GMGN win-rate filter. It reads the newest captured `gmgn_wallet_rank_snapshots` payload, preserves its `window`/`orderby`, takes the first N ranked wallets, and evaluates their stored trade history. UI `Top traders` changes N (25/50/100/250); `Period` changes the requested trade-history window (7/30/90 days).
- Decision: explain that GMGN-side filters affect which roster gets captured, while app-side controls affect only count and evaluation period. Do not imply the app can recreate GMGN's filter semantics from the stored win-rate field.

Verdicts are consistent, declining, recent_only, or insufficient.

2026-08-16 - Investigated GMGN CopyTrade rate-limit reports without changing code or data. Files inspected: src/copytrade/fetch.ts, src/copytrade/constants.ts, node_modules/gmgn-cli/skills/gmgn-portfolio/SKILL.md, and copytrade_fetch_runs in .data/crypto-research.sqlite. Finding: UTC 2026-08-17 has only 44 attempted requests across two rate-limited runs; UTC 2026-08-16 has 815 attempts across two runs (one cancelled, one rate-limited); UTC 2026-08-15 had 8,469 attempts across 12 runs. The two latest runs rate-limited after only 18 and 26 attempts, so this is not simply “too many calls today.” The newer fetch model does add one weight-3 wallet_stats request per wallet, and the daily insert cap does not reduce API page requests; it can still walk dense pages. The fetcher is serial with 200ms spacing (~5 calls/sec, below the documented ~6.7 weighted requests/sec ceiling), but GMGN may have an account/day or endpoint-specific quota not documented by the CLI. The CLI did not expose reset_at, so the app stored no reset deadline. Decision: do not retry now; wait for GMGN cooldown/quota reset. Before the next run, avoid repeated small retries and prefer one controlled run. Errors/unresolved: exact quota/reset cause cannot be distinguished from the CLI output; no application tests needed because this was read-only diagnosis. Next step: if rate limits recur after a cooldown, reduce avoidable stats calls for already-covered wallets and capture the raw CLI rate-limit response/reset_at for a definitive limit diagnosis.
2026-08-16 - Audited the persisted wait intervals between GMGN rate-limit events. Runs 14, 15, and 16 were the only rate-limited runs. Run 14 ended 2026-08-16 21:55:14.879Z after 20 requests; run 15 started 2h44m37s later and still rate-limited after 26 requests. Run 15 ended 00:40:06.918Z; run 16 started only 42s later and rate-limited after 18 requests. The latest rate limit ended at 00:40:59.536Z (2026-08-17 UTC). `rate_limited_until` is null in all three rows because the CLI did not surface reset metadata. Decision: the 42-second retry was clearly too soon and may have extended the ban; the 2h45m wait still did not clear it, which points to a longer cooldown or account/endpoint quota rather than only the documented short burst limiter. No code or data changed.
2026-08-16 - Implemented the "daily catch-up" fetch fix, per explicit user request, to stop CopyTrade re-walking already-known trade history on every run. Root cause (confirmed live before building): 14 of 25 roster wallets hit the 200-request cap before reaching 90 days back on run 12, and the fetcher had no resume point, so run 13 re-walked the same already-held stretch from scratch before making any new progress — that's why a re-fetch 2 days later still projected ~1h28m. Files changed: src/db/schema.ts (migration: `copytrade_wallet_coverage.resume_cursor TEXT`, `copytrade_fetch_runs.trades_duplicate`/`trades_daily_capped` counters), src/copytrade/constants.ts (`DAILY_TRADE_INSERT_CAP = 500`, documented as bounding stored volume per UTC day only — GMGN's activity endpoint has no time-range filter, confirmed via `gmgn-cli portfolio activity --help`, so a dense day still costs the same requests to page through, capping only changes what gets stored), src/copytrade/fetch.ts (two-phase per-wallet walk: catch-up from cursor=null always runs first to pick up new trades, then a one-shot jump to the wallet's saved `resume_cursor` — with a try/catch fallback to normal pagination if the resumed cursor is stale/rejected, since cursor reuse across days was never verified against GMGN and I didn't want a bad token to abort the whole run; `storeActivityPage` gained a `dailyCap` tracker seeded lazily from already-stored rows, so a resumed run doesn't need separate cross-run bookkeeping). Bug found and fixed via live testing (not assumption): the first live run showed `tradesFetched:0, tradesDailyCapped:900` — the daily cap check ran before the dedup check, so trades already stored by the pre-fix code were misreported as "capped" instead of "duplicate". Fixed by checking existence (`SELECT 1 ... WHERE dedup_key = ?`) before counting a capped-day trade as genuinely turned away; added a regression test. Test result: npx tsc -b/-p tsconfig.ui.json --noEmit both clean, npm test 223/223 passing (6 new tests: resume-cursor persist/clear, daily-cap basic behavior, cross-run seeding, duplicate-vs-capped regression, uncapped-unchanged). Verified live: schema migration auto-applied via tsx watch; a live 3-wallet run showed the daily cap and duplicate-vs-capped split working correctly against real re-fetched data (run 16: `tradesDuplicate:489, tradesDailyCapped:307`). Did NOT get to verify the resume-cursor jump itself firing on a second real run — GMGN rate-limited repeatedly during this session (see the two entries above, from a separate concurrent investigation) and I stopped retrying per that investigation's own "do not retry now" finding, so cursor-reuse-across-runs remains unverified in production, protected only by the try/catch fallback described above. 2026-08-17 - Fixed a real design flaw in Copy Simulation flagged by the user immediately after the feature above shipped: "Query more trades" only submitted one 150-target Dune batch per click, and with 3 wallets x up to 150 round trips x 2 legs each (~900 targets), that meant clicking it 5-6 times with no indication of how many clicks were left — an arbitrary batch-size cap I'd picked by mirroring dune/outcomes.ts's unrelated 25-signal limit, never actually justified for this feature's own scale. Files changed: src/copytrade/copySimulationDune.ts (raised MAX_TARGETS_PER_RUN from 150 to 300 - still one bounded Dune query, just a more efficient size for this no-fan-out query shape), src/copytrade/copySimulation.ts (runCopySimulationBatch now loops internally across as many 300-target batches as it takes to cover every currently-uncovered leg, up to a MAX_BATCHES_PER_CALL=15 runaway guard - several times more than the ~900-target ceiling this 3-wallet scope will ever need - returning `{runIds, targetsSubmitted, batchesRun, exhausted}` instead of a single runId so the caller knows definitively whether everything was covered), src/scripts/server.ts + ui/main.tsx (updated to the new response shape; button relabeled "Run simulation" since one click now means "run to completion," not "run one more batch"; the completion message reports batch count and only mentions running again if `exhausted` is genuinely true). Test result: npx tsc -b/-p tsconfig.ui.json --noEmit both clean, npm test 231/231 passing (no test changes needed - the looping change has no unit-testable pure-logic surface beyond what was already covered, and runCopySimulationBatch itself was never network-tested, consistent with measureDuneOutcomes's own precedent), npm run build:ui clean. Verified live against the real database: a single "Run simulation" call submitted the remaining 115 uncovered targets in exactly 1 batch (`batchesRun:1, exhausted:false`) and all 3 wallets now show real simulated medians (DxM -4.5%, 7JF +52.6%, H9T -3.8%) where 2 of 3 previously showed "-" - confirmed in both the API response and the live browser UI. 2026-08-17 - Re-onboarded on the current Historical Copy Simulation logic. Files inspected: src/copytrade/copySimulation.ts, src/copytrade/copySimulationDune.ts, src/scripts/server.ts, ui/main.tsx. Decision: treat the section as a read-only, dynamically recomputed simulation for the current top three Winners, not a frozen roster and not live trading. It pairs each sell with the last prior buy for the same wallet/token, considers the latest 150 round trips per wallet, queries Dune for each buy/sell at a 15-second delayed timestamp, accepts only matches within 300 seconds, and reports wallet median versus simulated median, delay cost, copied/missed trades, worst return, and drawdown. Defaults are 1% GMGN fee + 0.5% slippage per side and 0.002 SOL gas per transaction; gas is reported separately and is not converted into the percentage return. Existing completed simulation matches are append-only and reused by trade id; the Run simulation button batches uncovered legs up to 300 targets per Dune query and loops to completion. No code or data changed; no tests run.
2026-08-17 - Reviewed Historical Copy Simulation fairness against live results. Current coverage: RC 97/150 copied (53 missed), 7JF 127/150 (23 missed), H9T 146/150 (4 missed); simulated medians are -4.54%, +52.60%, and -3.83% respectively. Strengths: current top-three selection is deterministic from the existing Winners gates; Dune matches are real delayed on-chain observations; missing/stale matches are visible and never converted to zero; assumptions are explicit; raw runs are append-only and deduplicated. Fairness findings: (1) major — wallet median uses all 150 round trips while simulated median uses only copied trades, so the two medians have different denominators; missing trades can bias the simulated result, especially for RC. Compare wallet and simulated returns on the same copied subset and report coverage separately. (2) major — this is in-sample: current Winners are selected using historical performance and then simulated on the same recent history; it estimates execution drag, not out-of-sample winner quality. (3) moderate — fixed 0.5% slippage does not depend on trade size, liquidity, or pool; fixed gas is reported in SOL but omitted from percentage returns, so small trades are not economically comparable. (4) moderate — latest 150 round trips per wallet is a recency slice, not a random/full-history sample; equal caps help comparability but can overrepresent a recent regime. (5) moderate — last-buy-before-sell pairing does not model quantities, partial fills, overlapping positions, or concurrent capital; the sequential drawdown curve is therefore illustrative, not a true portfolio simulation. (6) moderate — nearest Dune trade is a market proxy, not a guaranteed copier fill or same-route quote. Decision: keep the feature for execution-sensitivity research, but do not call its current medians a fair ranking of the three wallets until paired-denominator coverage and an explicit in-sample label are added. No code or data changed; no tests run.

2026-08-17 - Grounded two Copy Simulation assumptions against GMGN's own published Copy Trade docs, which the user pasted in full, and added a cost dimension the docs revealed was missing. Findings: (1) the docs give no absolute copy-trade latency figure — only a relative one ("Lightning Mode ... about 2s faster ... under the same parameters") — so DEFAULT_COPIER_DELAY_SECONDS stays at 15 as a labeled assumption; I did not invent a "real" number from a doc that doesn't contain one. (2) The docs do give an exact, authoritative fee formula: "One copytrade transaction = buying/selling amount + Gas priority fee + 1% GMGN handling fee, no other factors" — this confirms DEFAULT_FEE_BPS=100 as documented rather than merely assumed (comment updated to cite the source), and reveals a real gap: gas priority fee is a FIXED SOL cost per transaction (GMGN's docs: 0.002-0.006 SOL, recommending the low end to avoid "profitable trades that still lose money to gas"), not a percentage — my existing slippageBps model couldn't represent that at all. Asked the user how to handle USD conversion (no grounded SOL/USD price source in this project); they chose to report gas cost in SOL only, un-converted, rather than invent a price constant. Files changed: src/copytrade/copySimulation.ts (added `DEFAULT_GAS_PRIORITY_FEE_SOL = 0.002`, cited to the docs' recommended low end; `CopySimulationTradeResult.gasFeeSol` — 2x the per-tx rate per copied round trip, buy+sell each pay it, null for anything not actually simulated; `CopySimulationWalletReport.totalGasFeeSol`; `gasPriorityFeeSolPerTx` added to the assumptions block; deliberately kept separate from simulatedReturnPercent rather than blended into it, since mixing a documented SOL cost into a price-ratio percentage without a grounded conversion would reintroduce exactly the kind of unmeasured assumption this feature otherwise avoids), src/copytrade/copySimulationDune.ts (updated MAX_TARGETS_PER_RUN's comment only, no logic change), ui/main.tsx + styles.css (new "Gas cost (copied trades)" row per wallet card, assumptions line now states the GMGN-fee and gas-fee sources).
